3. They save what they can: the city, the gathering, and the people who matter to them.
4. The Watcher stays over Kurose. Nothing has stopped its escalation, and it goes on probing the city's defences, without malice (LEDGER #41). *(Canon)* Abraxas is beside it. *(Proposal)*
5. Anabala and Rupiah tell them how Kortos was closed: Sarenrae sent the four back from Nirvana with a way to close it, and it worked because Kortos was one dropped container. What the Watcher built is far bigger. Only the fuel's makers know how to stop that much, so both crises need the mitflit home world (LEDGER #40). *(Canon)* The founders' players can play that story as a flashback (Appendix E). *(Proposal)*
6. The search ship lifts, with the gathering aboard. Who goes with it, and who stays to hold the city, is theirs to decide.

**Choices**

- **Who goes, and who stays?** The Book V crew, and the people who hold the city while they're gone (fork).

**What they can find out**

- The answer is on the mitflit home world.

**GM notes** *(GM-only)*

- *(Flag)* What exactly Sarenrae sent them back with is open. It belongs to the flashback, and it closes the old campaign, which ended in Nirvana.
- *(Proposal)* The search ship's own drive was never touched: the weapon was built from exotic matter taken out of it. That's why the ship can still lift.
- Where Manako is by now, and whether he's alive, is decided closer to the time (LEDGER #42). If the party befriended him or turned him away from violence, perhaps he's helpful; he may already be dead.

**The Watcher:** It stays over Kurose, still escalating, and it keeps everything it learned from them (LEDGER #41).

**Where it leaves them:** The answer is on the mitflit home world.

## §11 Book V: The Long Way Home (Chapters 16–20)

*Command staff of a starship, with nobody above them.*

- **The question:** What do we do with it?
- **The party:** A ship and its crew of thousands. Each player commands their own part of it (a Frame wing, a department, a deck), so nobody's turn goes into a shared token. *(Proposal)*
- **Their jobs:** Theirs to set.
- **Against them:** Distance, and the clock at home: the rift growing, and the Watcher still probing the city's defences.
- **Deliberately loose:** The home world is yours, and so is the ending. No spaceship combat: every fight is at personnel scale or Frame scale. *(Canon)*

### Chapter 16: Lift

*A starship.* The search ship, restored and crewed by the mitflits, leaves Golarion with the six in command and nobody above them.

**At the table**

1. *(Proposal)* Each player takes command of their own part of the ship: a Frame wing, a department, a deck.
2. *(Proposal)* Who's aboard: Anabala, Hyuck as test pilot, Shinobu if she chooses, the stray, and Rand, stowed away.
3. The fragment of Mother comes too, out of its museum case. With the search ship itself, it's the only proof they have that they speak for the generation ship's descendants (LEDGER #41).
4. *(Proposal)* Who stays to hold Kurose: Rupiah, Shucks, the Deputy Director, the Commander if he's still there, Bobbin, and Vetch, who is still hazing the next intake. They hold it against the rift, and against the Watcher, which stayed.
5. The first decisions with nobody above them: supplies, where to go first, and the mitflits' own politics aboard.

**Where it leaves them:** Nobody above them.

### Chapter 17: Derelicts

*A starship.* Boarding actions on ships that fell at Golarion, the reason ships fall there, and news from home arriving as a clock.

**At the table**

1. *(Proposal)* Boarding actions and away missions on ships that fell at Golarion over the ages: fights at personnel scale and Frame scale.
2. Their logs explain why ships fall at Golarion.
3. The mitflits' politics aboard.
4. Reports from home arrive as a clock: the rift is growing, and the Watcher is still probing the city's defences.
5. *(Proposal)* An optional flashback: the generation ship's arrival, played as its crew, with Mother whole.

**Where it leaves them:** The rift at home is growing.

### Chapter 18: Landfall

*A starship.* The mitflit home world, and the High mitflits.

**At the table**

1. The mitflit home world. *(Canon)* The High mitflits are elegant, unblemished and aloof.
2. They present the only proof they have that they speak for the generation ship's descendants: the search ship, and the fragment of Mother (LEDGER #41).
3. *(Flag)* What the wild mitflits find there, and what the High mitflits make of them, is yours.

**Where it leaves them:** On the home world, looking for the answer.

### Chapter 19: The Problem at Home

*A starship.* A unique, extreme problem on the home world, where the answer to both crises lives.

**At the table**

1. *(Canon)* A unique, extreme problem on the home world, and the answer to both crises lives here.
2. *(Proposal)* Seeds, take any or none: the home world lost ships too, and knows what an eye without a mind becomes; the High mitflits don't want the wild ones back; the gods' reason for felling ships becomes clear; a ship's mind has a name at home, and Mother's is known.

**Where it leaves them:** Holding the answer to both crises, with a rift to close at home.

### Chapter 20: The Return

*A starship.* Home to a city that held, to close the rift and settle what becomes of the Watcher.

**At the table**

1. They come home to a Kurose that held: whoever stayed behind has been holding the city against the rift and the Watcher's children.
2. They close the rift, with what they found on the home world.
3. *(Flag)* What becomes of the Watcher isn't planned yet, and gets built as the ending gets closer (LEDGER #41). Perhaps it disperses for good; perhaps it survives and becomes harmless; perhaps the mitflits come back and scoop it out of the air to take it home.
4. Rand's archive is published as testimony.
5. *(Flag)* Perhaps the only way to undo Second Impact is the Gap, and the price of closing the rift is that nobody remembers it (LEDGER #33).

**Where it leaves them:** The end is theirs.

## §12 Who's moving, Book by Book

| | Book I | Book II | Book III | Book IV | Book V |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **The Watcher** | Copies, a first new kind (to rethink), Breakers: only where Sap was released | Seekers, then G4s: their machines, then their people; it watches the convoy (8) | The Directorate itself, then the Reel | People-shaped; takes the exotic matter (13); feeds itself; builds a weapon (14); Second Impact (15) | Still over Kurose, still escalating; its fate is open |
| **Manako** | Patient | Patient → Delighted | Delighted → Cornered | Decided closer to the time (LEDGER #42); drafted as Frightened → broken | Decided closer to the time (LEDGER #42) |
| **The four lieutenants** | The Soldier (3); the Fixer and the masked Young One (4) | Kiri, at her show (6) | Each hands over the next step: the Fixer's books (9), the Soldier's arrest (11), the Young One unmasked and Kiri's succession (12) | Whoever is still free, at Second Impact (15) | — |
| **The Directorate** | Vetch and his paperwork | The Deputy Director; it reads their mail; the convoy under lockdown (8) | The Commander; the sirens; the ban on lenses | *(Proposal)* Its leadership falls to them | The people who stay and hold the city |
| **Baldwin *(GM-only)*** | Impossible weapons in gang hands | Mitflits missing from the gathering | Croglang; money above the crime lords | *(Flag)* May step out (parked) | Open |
| **Abraxas *(GM-only)*** | Unmentioned | Unmentioned | Unmentioned | Gets out, beside the Watcher | Separated from it, or not |
| **The founders** | Shucks, from day one | Hyuck, hidden; Anabala arrives; Rupiah on Founding Day | Hyuck unmasked; First Impact was theirs, and the Watcher is news to them | Anabala at the vault | Who goes, and who stays |
| **Rand** | On paper: the countersignature, the override, the chalk (1); an early requisition (4) | Unseen | Its archive reaches the sceptics (11) | — | A stowaway; the archive published |

## §13 The forks

The first seven are the v4 bible's own turning points, placed on the twenty chapters; the last three are mine. Everywhere else, acknowledge the players' choices cheaply and often, and spend real branching only here (`design/agency-and-player-choice.md`).

| The choice | Ch | What follows either way |
|---|---|---|
| Find Nozomi early? | 2 | Cicada's argument arrives from someone they trust, before they have any answer to it; or they meet her later, officially, with the Directorate listening. |
| Feed the stray? | 5 on | It grows, shaped by whoever raised it, and later it's the proof that the Watcher can be raised differently; or it stays small, and hungry. |
| Tell the city about the sky? | 6–9 | An early sighting, and a big win for Cicada; or the Directorate's reflex to hide things becomes their own. |
| Turn Nao in? | 9 | Trusted by the Directorate and nobody else; or harbouring a cultist. |
| Expose Nobu, or use him? | 9 | The column's money stops, and so does every repair they rely on; or they're complicit, and know it. |
| Break the sit-in? | 10 | Bobbin in mortal danger, and the mitflits won't forget; or the city goes undefended for a while, and people notice. |
| Take Manako alive? | 12 | Redemption as labour, and the Watcher keeps its only steering; or it runs on its purpose alone. |
| *(Proposal)* Use its eyes? | 14 | Win everything nearer home, and teach it that watching everyone is what good people do. |
| *(Proposal)* Who goes, and who stays? | 15 | The Book V crew, and the people who hold the city while they're gone. |
| *(Flag)* What becomes of it? | 20 | Open (LEDGER #41): dispersed for good, harmless, or scooped up and taken home. |

## §14 Calls I made in this version *(Proposals, except where your answer is noted)*

Every contradiction I found reading version 6 against the sources, and what I did about it; where you've answered since, it says so. Where two sources disagreed, I took the reading I think you'd pick. Overturn any of them.

- **Why the six share the marker.** Version 6 said "by coincidence"; Chapter 1's built scenes have them put in one section on purpose. Both are true: they carry it by coincidence (your answer, LEDGER #31), and the screen put them together because of it (`design/induction.md`). The line in LEDGER #20 and Intake Medicals' notes calling it "a genuine statistical surprise" now means six carriers in one intake, not six in one section.
- **What Manako saw, and when.** Version 6 said he lost his career for seeing the sky forty years ago. Per your answer (LEDGER #39) and his dossier: a spill showed a tentacle in his lab 44 years ago, Nobu wouldn't back him up, and that is what ruined him. The sky came about four years later, through his own lens.
- **Hulda Brack is a nuar, and alien.** Her brief said "non-human, but not an alien", and your PF2e rule (LEDGER #39) makes nuar alien. My first call made her a minotaur to keep her Golarion's own; you overturned it (LEDGER #44). She stays a nuar: aliens are outsiders here, glamoured clothing is on sale on the high street, and she refuses it.
- **When Cicada began.** The timeline says at Manako's conference, 38 years ago; version 6 and LEDGER #28 say after Aoi died, 15 years ago. The Casebook uses both: a small, peaceful circle around him after the conference, which became the movement after Aoi.
- **Where the Chapter 4 technique comes from.** Version 6 had the old man teach it in person. His dossier has it come from his discredited papers, so the party retraces his steps without knowing the papers are his (they know his name from Chapter 1, not his work). That's what makes his handwriting land in Chapter 12, so the Casebook uses the papers.
- **How often they meet Manako.** The sources say perhaps four times in the whole campaign; version 6 had him in person most chapters. The Casebook keeps him to the tea house (1), messages if one of them has his number, perhaps the lens lesson (7), and the accusation (12); after that, where he is is decided closer to the time (LEDGER #42).
- **The Soldier doesn't hand them Hori.** Hori's file says the two were at the same incident and neither knows the other exists. The Soldier tells his own side of the day, and the police file names Hori.
- **Kiri.** An elderly illusionist on the private-show circuit, not a street magician: showy public magic is frowned on in Kurose. Her "ledger" had no source, so it's now what the dossier says: she is quietly planning who takes over from Manako.
- **Nao.** Version 6 had a Cicada dossier turn up in Nao's hands. The dossier has the party find Nao's own file, as one of the Faithful.
- **The Young One.** A year or two ahead of them, and a little competitive rather than friendly.
- **Who kills, and when.** In Book I the party meets only the Soldier's squad, which doesn't kill. The Cadre as a whole will, and the first fight to the death is at the column in Chapter 9.
- **Bobbin stays.** Version 6 put him on the ship. His line has always been that he refuses to leave.
- **Vetch stays.** Version 6 made him a junior officer aboard the ship. His arc is that he's still there at the end, doing the same to the next intake, unredeemed.
- **Anabala isn't on the base.** Your words were that the founders all work on the base except Anabala and Rand. She arrives in Kurose with the gathering in Chapter 6.
- **"Oda" is the Commander again.** The name only ever appeared in the old v4 bible, and his data file has no name.
- **Nozomi, officially.** Interviewing detainees unlocks at Clearance 2, so the official interview is in Chapter 4. Finding her in Chapter 2 is the rule-bending fork.
- **The sea wall.** It's in Episode Two (Slack Water), not Episode One.
- **The lens.** Version 6 said it "works where Sap is thick". The sources say it always works, and what varies is whether the Watcher is attending that place. The GM notes now say plainly that the glass is treated with Sap.
- **Contacts in Book I.** Version 6 had contacts forming "where attention pools" in Chapter 4, which is how they behave from Book II. In the Casebook, Book I's children form only where Sap was released; what Chapter 4 finds is that its attention gathers at a site before anything forms there.
- **The prophecy and the call.** The prophecy came soon after First Impact, when Rupiah asked the gods; the move to Kurose is what happened 150 years ago. And the search ship was calling before anyone dug; the digging only makes the call louder.
- **Tags.** Mother as the ship's mind, the statue being Hyuck, and Manako as the Watcher's first supplier are proposals, not canon, and are now marked so. The ship's bridge answering the marker had no source and is marked as a proposal; the fragment "asking for something it has lost" had none either, and is gone.
- **Small ones.** "The sergeant" in the Watcher's voice is Vetch (there's no sergeant anywhere else). "The players' own old characters" is four players' old characters: Jamie and Anne didn't play the founders. Founding Day is what Scene 0 shows (the painting in every window, Rupiah cutting the ribbon) rather than a balcony and a singing crowd. The Proto-Claimant fights its reflection in the water, as the earlier draft had it.
- **Found in the built scenes.** Fixed after your fifth answers (LEDGER #44): Assembly no longer calls civil defence national service; Unidentified Contact's two times agree (0841, with the alert at 0846); Inert 12 is a century old; `manako.json` and `nobu.json` lost their stale notes; and plants 2, 3 and 10 are in the built scenes. Fixed in the sweep for this version: Episode Two happens the day after Episode One, but School Period Two, The Hammer and Zero Six Hundred said "a fortnight ago", and First Contact called the proving ground "yesterday" when it's the same morning; The Long Afternoon still had the tea-house book's Thursday; The Night Off's route to Manako now includes his number; Kit Issue still pointed at a "Chapter Six reveal"; and Manako's file still had a "no inquest". Kept on purpose: `data/encounters/c1e3-the-call-out.json`, a drafted Episode 3 fight nothing uses.
- **The sweep for version 8.** Beyond the built scenes, the sweep fixed: Rand, accepted wherever it still said "awaiting her read"; Inert 12's last "two-century-old" mentions; conscription, which the charter no longer mentions (seventeen is the kingdom's own national service, separate from the Directorate); "the Observer", which the mitflits no longer say "flatly and without fear" but feel as a comforting presence; the old candidate causes of Second Impact, rewritten where they still read as current; Hulda's notes; the Watcher's own file (Second Impact is its weapon, it stays over Kurose, and its end is open); The Errand's scene card, which now describes the workshop; Manako's movement V, marked as one possible end; the Mystery Ledger's summary of which clues are planted; and open questions the rounds answered, closed in Rand's, the Section Officer's and Nozomi's files. The LEDGER, the frozen design bible and resolved open questions keep their history as written.

## §15 Gaps I filled *(Proposals, except where marked accepted)*

Places where the story had nothing, and what I put there. All of these are mine: keep, change or cut. The ones you have accepted say so.

- **A wrong answer to start inside.** The Directorate's official story, that someone magically summoned a monster (LEDGER #35), becomes the party's working theory in Book I. Every early attack supports it, because in Book I the Watcher's children can only form where the cult has released Sap.
- **The horror needed staging.** The sky full of eyes is seen in three steps, each bigger than the last: a hall at Kiri's show (Chapter 6), the dig (Chapter 8), and the whole city (Chapter 10). See §4.3.
- **A reason the Directorate falls to them.** In Chapter 13 the party breaks into the deepest vault looking for information (your way in, LEDGER #32), and the Watcher's children follow them through and take the exotic matter the convoy brought in. Losing the most dangerous thing on Golarion breaks the Directorate's leadership: the Commander resigns, taking responsibility, and the Deputy Director, the founders and the city put their weight behind the six, who have shown the ability and judgement for it (your answer, LEDGER #42).
- **The surveillance theme needed a temptation as well as a threat.** In Chapter 14 someone offers them the Watcher's eyes. *(Flag)* On hold until it has a mechanism (LEDGER #36).
- **The four lieutenants needed exits that hand the party the next step.** The Fixer's books (Chapter 9), the Soldier's arrest and his account of the day the Envoy came (Chapter 11), and the Young One's unmasking and Kiri's succession plan (Chapter 12) each point the party somewhere new.
- **Every Book needed a breather.** Book I has the night off and school already. I added Founding Day in Book II, a dinner with the sceptics in Book III, and graduation in Book IV.
- **Chapter 1's clues had no payoffs.** Appendix B gives each one a chapter, and says which ones the built episodes don't yet plant.
- **The Watcher needed a pace.** A new generation of its children roughly every two chapters, each built to beat whatever the party relied on last (Appendix C).
- **The charter needed a reason to be looked at.** Accepted (LEDGER #44). With its painted-out paragraph cut (LEDGER #41), its four signatures are the clue: Shucks's old scrawl, which is the hand Rand writes in; HYUCK in capitals; and a script nobody here reads, which is Anabala's. It's the check in Intake Medicals.
- **The Transparency Advocate needed a new refusal.** Accepted (LEDGER #44). They used to ask to see the unredacted charter. Now they have asked three times for the inventory of what the Directorate keeps in deep storage, and been told it's held above their clearance. It plants the vaults (LEDGER #40) without saying what's in them.
- **Manako's one true thing needed rewriting.** Accepted (LEDGER #44). His Errand speech was about the painted-over paragraph. Now it's about the vaults: whatever the Directorate digs up goes into one and is never spoken of again. True, and secretive rather than evil (LEDGER #41).
- **The Carrying needed a name and details.** The practice is yours (LEDGER #41). The name, the skull that carries a spirit between bodies, the bearer, the wrestle, riding and wearing, and Hyuck's count of bodies are mine: `ideas/story/bible/characters/hyuck.md`.
- **The stray needed somewhere to come from.** Nothing they beat ever dies; it drains home. The stray is the one crumb that doesn't, after a fight in Chapter 5. It replaces a "lab spill" nobody had explained.
- **Rand needed a reason.** Accepted (LEDGER #46). It does what Shucks won't. The night after Intake Medicals it read Shucks's notes, as it reads everything on his desk, and found six carriers of the marker in one intake, which it has never seen. Shucks would do nothing: a founder openly steering six trainees draws the attention the marker mustn't get. The hand has no position to protect. So it countersigned their requisition in Episode One (and enjoyed making the rules bite the man who hazed them), and when the fortnight came it stacked a Medical order on top: a fitness familiarisation, Frame and vehicle, which a medical department can order and nobody can refuse. It chalked 'Same six. Tomorrow.' to keep them together. Its reason for hurrying is its own paper trail: something in the city is wrong and getting worse, and Gen 2 carriers are the most valuable people on the base. That week, three things form on the harbour road. At the end of Episode Two Shucks sees his own old handwriting on the order, knows his hand wrote it, and walks into the Deputy Director's office to make it stick.
- **The override's names needed an order.** They're in the order the six went through Intake Medicals, which is the order their files sit in Medical's cabinet. A player who remembers the queue can notice.
- **The Errand's first stop needed a building.** Your new shape (LEDGER #45) needed a technical workshop. My first pick, the Instrument Shop on the works, was about 600 m from the tea house, and you withdrew it (LEDGER #46): it's now an instrument makers' workshop a few streets from the tea house, and the tea house sits in a district where Manako has a lot of influence. Neither is done until you site them in the gazetteer (req-5, req-6). The General Store's shelf clue moved to the workshop's stores cupboard. The Foundry scene still opens on the General Store's map and art; the workshop's art prompt is in `data/campaign/art-status.md`.
- **Chapter 12 needed his handwriting.** With the letters gone, he writes the vial's part number on the back of the bill in Chapter 1, and the party keeps it.
- **Second Impact needed a road the players can see coming.** Your answer makes it the Watcher's weapon (LEDGER #40). I gave it three visible steps: it watches the convoy (Chapter 8), takes the exotic matter from the vault (Chapter 13), and builds the weapon over the dig, where a lens shows a violet the founders know from Kortos (Chapter 14).

What I deliberately left alone: the home world's problem, when Baldwin steps out and how he has lived this long, Jamie and Anne's flashback characters, and whether the Gap is how it ends.

## §16 Still yours

- **The dilemma's temptation.** It needs a mechanism, or it goes (LEDGER #36).
- **Chapter 2's first generation (G1).** Cut (LEDGER #46), and still to design before Chapter 2 is written (GitHub #206). Whatever it is, it forms only where Sap has been released, like everything in Book I.
- **The Errand's two places.** Site the tea house and the workshop in the gazetteer (req-5, req-6), and make the workshop's art from the prompt in `data/campaign/art-status.md` (GitHub #164).
- **Mother.** The museum fragment is hers, and it's the party's proof on the home world (LEDGER #41). Still open: her voice, and who it talks to (N21, needed before Chapter 6, when the fragment is first seen); and whether a piece of her is in every Gen 2.
- **The shuttle under Kortos.** The founders were fetching fuel for the generation ship's shuttle when the accident made the Watcher, and they never reached it. It's still buried under Kortos. Does it matter again, perhaps as Anabala's way home (N18, `open-questions.json#kortos-shuttle`)? Needed before Book IV.
- **Sarenrae's help.** What exactly she sent the four back from Nirvana with, to close Kortos (LEDGER #40). It belongs to the Book IV flashback, and it closes the old campaign.
- **The home world's problem.** Book V is built to wait for it.
- **Manako in Books IV and V.** Where he is, and whether he's alive: decided closer to the time, from how the table treated him (LEDGER #42).
- **The Watcher's fate.** Dispersed for good, harmless, or scooped out of the air and taken home: to be built as the ending gets closer (LEDGER #41).
- **Baldwin.** When he steps out, and how he has lived this long (parked).
- **The stray.** Kept (LEDGER #42). Whether it works at the table is still to find out; where it comes from (my crumb that didn't drain home) is a proposal.
- **Jamie and Anne's flashback characters.** For the flashbacks between Books.
- **The Gap.** Whether it's how the story ends.

## Appendix A: three roads to every conclusion

Every conclusion the story needs, with three independent ways to reach it, so no discovery hangs on one roll on one night. Chapter numbers in brackets; the last column is the earliest it can land.

| What they can conclude | Road 1 | Road 2 | Road 3 | Earliest |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| The cult isn't summoning them | An attack with no Sap anywhere (5) | Its attention gathers at a site before anything forms there (4) | The stray: a piece of it in someone's pocket (5 on) | 5 |
| Something reads what they write | A fight opens against the plan in their messages (5) | A stranger says a line only ever typed privately (7) | The form they signed on day one (8) | 8 |
| Cicada has an author | The Fixer's books (9) | The Young One's misquotes | Kiri's plans for who takes over (12) | 9 |
| The author is Manako | His handwriting: the bill he wrote the part number on (1), against Cicada's papers | The sealed lab records of his disgrace, which fit what the Chapter 1 knowledge check can tell them | The revenge list: Shucks and Nobu | 12 |
| What Sap is | Inert 12 in the Directorate's catalogue (7) | Shinobu, splashed with it, walking away from a lorry (6) | The column (9) | 9 |
| What the Watcher is | The museum fragment, if Mother speaks | The Kortos survey in Rand's archive: the generation ship's navigation and sensor system, missing since First Impact | The mitflits' "Observer": the presence they feel keeping them on course, set beside what a lens shows | 11 |
| The Envoy was a greeting | Hori | The Soldier's side of the day | Manako's own account | 11 |
| How they fight is what it becomes | The Reel (11) | Each generation answering their last tactic | The stray, raised kindly | 11 |
| Who signed their roster | Shucks's hand is a prosthetic, and the name is Rand | The handwriting matches Shucks's old signature on the founding charter (1) | Catching it chalking the board at night | 1 on |
| *(Proposal)* The Watcher is building something from exotic matter | The vault theft: what the convoy brought in is gone (13) | Through a lens, a violet glow taking shape over the dig (14) | The founders know that violet from Kortos (14) | 14 |
| First Impact was the founders' accident | The Kortos survey in Rand's archive (11) | Mother's fragment, who was there | The founders themselves, asked straight | 11 |

## Appendix B: Chapter 1's clues, paid off *(Proposal)*

The Mystery Ledger (`ideas/story/blank-inheritance-ch1-v2.md` §5) left every payoff open. These assign them, and say honestly which clues the built episodes actually plant.

| # | What Chapter 1 shows them | Pays off in | What it turns out to be |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | All six flag irregular on the blood screen | 4 | Shucks tells them: it's the marker a Gen 2 needs. |
| 2 | Nozomi: a name half-heard at Intake Medicals, and the BROUGHT FORWARD stamp on every letter | 2, 4 | The first pilot, detained, and the show that took her. (Also planted now: her name struck through on the Hangar Two board in Episode Two, LEDGER #44.) |
| 3 | A tarp in Hangar Four | 4 | A Gen 2 prototype. (Planted: glimpsed through the Hangar Four shutter in Episode Two's Familiarisation, LEDGER #44.) |
| 4 | The same hull breach on every Gen 2 prototype | 5 | The core making its seat. (Not planted; Gen 2 only, or cut.) |
| 5 | The founding charter in the lobby | 1, 6, 12 | Cut as a mystery (LEDGER #41): nothing on it is painted out. Its four signatures are the clue instead (accepted, LEDGER #44): Shucks's old scrawl, which is the hand Rand writes in; HYUCK in capitals; and a script nobody here reads, which is Anabala's. |
| 6 | Violet stencils at head height | 4, 6 | Where Cicada will gather next; and the show. |
| 7 | Gaps in the instrument makers' stores cupboard | 9 | The column's raw materials, stolen in the heist loop. (Not planted: cut from The Errand in version 9, where it had no payoff yet.) |
| 8 | Hyuck's protocol | 12 | *(Flag)* Gen 2 only, to be pinned down later. (Not planted.) |
| 9 | Dogs, birds, the street, the rooftop | 1 (Episode Two) | Animals sense the Watcher's nanoparticles, perhaps as a high buzz that upsets them (LEDGER #43). *(Canon)* The street drops to the harbour road, where the three Proto-Claimants form. *(Proposal)* |
| 10 | The girl in the news, on the canteen radio | 6 | Shinobu, walking away from a lorry. (Planted: the Canteen's read-aloud now carries her father on a phone-in, unnamed, LEDGER #44.) |
| 11 | A Frame moving more gently than ordered | 7 | *(Proposal)* Hyuck, testing a Gen 2 prototype. (Not planted: The Road In's Frame sighting is cut.) |
| 12 | Cultists helping people up while being arrested | 2, 11 | The Faithful's refusal to resist, and the Soldier's rule that nobody dies. (First seen in Chapter 2.) |
| 13 | The lens that mostly doesn't work | 6 | It always works; what varies is whether the Watcher is there. (First seen at the show.) |

## Appendix C: the Watcher's children, generation by generation

*(Proposal)* Each generation answers the party's last reliable approach with one new trick: harder means losing a tactic they counted on, not more hit points. They're built to party level with ordinary SF2E XP rules, and nothing is ever killed: a beaten child drains home, and every fight is data.

| | Ch | Name | What it has learned | What the party meets |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| G0 | 1–2 | Proto-Claimants | Nothing yet | Copies of shapes it has seen: the statue, a reflection |
| G1 | 2–3 | *(Flag)* To rethink | Things that move are alive | Cut (LEDGER #46); a new first generation is still to design |
| G2 | 3–4 | Breakers | Frames matter | Children that pry at the same weak points on every clanker |
| G3 | 5–6 | Seekers | Frames have pilots | Hatch-openers, drawn hard to Gen 2 |
| G4 | 6–8 | Hand-sized | Pilots have people | Small children at the shop, the sibling's school, the teacher's door |
| G5 | 9–10 | Institution-breakers | There are institutions | Attacks on several Directorate sites at once: logistics, the plotting floor, the sirens |
| G6 | 11–12 | The Reel | The party's own tactics | Their moves, improved; every fight opens by replaying their last one |
| G7 | 13–14 | Envoys | There are people | People-shaped children that talk, and hold for days |
| G8 | 14 on | Makers | It can feed itself, and build what its makers built | Children that make Sap: the runaway. Then, from exotic matter, a weapon (15) |

## Appendix D: "How did it know?"

Each step is earned by something the Watcher really observed, and it only ever uses the fiction: what the characters wrote, never anything real about a player.

| Ch | What happens at the table |
|---|---|
| 2–4 | A Claimant rolls Recall Knowledge against a PC. It reads as a GM slip. |
| 5–6 | A fight opens against the plan they made in their messages the night before. |
| 7–8 | A stranger says a line one of them only ever typed privately. |
| 9–10 | A garbled phone notification quotes a message from days ago. |
| 11–12 | The Reel: a fight opens with their last fight played back, what they said on the net included. |
| 13–14 | An Envoy greets a PC by the nickname their friends use in messages. |

## Appendix E: flashbacks *(Proposal)*

Played by Liam, Pippa, Craig and Steve as the founders, between Books. Jamie and Anne need flashback characters of their own.

- **Late in Book I, after Chapter 3.** Baldwin Manor and the shadow siege: the grudge, and the hunter's horse Hyuck stole and made into armour.
- **In Book II, around Chapter 7.** The jungle ship and First Impact, ending on the catch.
- **In Book III.** Anabala building Gen 2, and deciding what the Directorate will be told about its core. Or the founding itself: the four signing the charter.
- **In Book IV, at Second Impact (Chapter 15).** Nirvana and the temple: Sarenrae sends the four back, and they close the Kortos rift at Pharasma's temple. It is the old campaign's unfinished ending.
- **In Book V.** The generation ship's arrival, played as its crew, with Mother whole.
